Evaluating Options:**<br><br>- **A**: Reverses domain order (synthetase N-term, amidase C-term) — inconsistent with the observed GATase motif in the N-terminal region.<br>- **B**: A single domain carrying both functions is unlikely given the clear two-domain architecture of a 580-aa protein.<br>- **C**: N-terminal amidase + C-terminal synthetase; separating domains increases amidase activity. <td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which model best describes the organization of this bifunctional enzyme and the effect of separating its catalytic domains?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> The N-terminal domain is the synthetase and the C-terminal domain is the amidase; separating them reduces synthetase activity.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> The N-terminal domain is an amidase and the C-terminal domain is a synthetase; both activities require the domains to remain joined.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> The N-terminal domain is an amidase and the C-terminal domain is a synthetase; each domain can function independently, and separation strongly increases amidase activity.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> The N-terminal domain is an amidase and the C-terminal domain is a synthetase; separation strongly increases synthetase activity while leaving amidase activity unchanged.<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_molecular_function_0063:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Analyzing the protein sequence:**<br>- The N-terminal region contains motifs consistent with a Glutamine Amidotransferase Class I (GATase-I) domain, including the characteristic catalytic triad residues. The GATase domain hydrolyzes glutamine to generate ammonia, which is then channeled to the synthetase domain for ATP-dependent amide transfer.<br><br>**Evaluating domain separation effects:**<br>Studies on FGAMS and related bifunctional amidotransferase-synthetase enzymes have demonstrated:<br>- Both domains **can function independently** when separated (the synthetase uses exogenous ammonia).<br>- Separation **increases amidotransferase activity** because the GATase domain is no longer constrained by the ammonia channeling mechanism.
